\displaystyle\theta_{m}=-\Delta_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}+G^{\prime}(\psi_{m})+\alpha\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi_{m}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.\penalty\ on\penalty\ }\Sigma,

(6.5b)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}K\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi_{m}=\alpha\psi_{m}-\phi_{m},&\text{if\penalty\ }K\in[0,\infty),\\
\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi_{m}=0,&\text{if\penalty\ }K=\infty,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.\penalty\ on\penalty\ }\Sigma,

(6.5c)

and

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})|_{t=0}=\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}),\hskip 10.00002pt(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})|_{t=0}=(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in\penalty\ }\Omega\times\Gamma.

(6.6)

In particular, because of (
6.5
), the pair
(\mu_{m},\theta_{m})
satisfies the weak formulation

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu_{m}\eta\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\vartheta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}\nabla\phi_{m}\cdot\nabla\eta+F^{\prime}(\phi_{m})\eta\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\vartheta+G^{\prime}(\psi_{m})\vartheta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\chi(K)\int_{\Gamma}(\alpha\psi_{m}-\phi_{m})(\alpha\vartheta-\eta)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

a.e. on
[0,T]
for all
(\eta,\vartheta)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K,\alpha}
. The existence of such an approximate solution will be established in the next subsection.

6.2  Existence of approximate solutions

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
be arbitrary.
We employ a fixed-point argument to prove the existence of an approximate solution
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m},\phi_{m},\psi_{m},\mu_{m},\theta_{m})
satisfying
(
6.2
)-(
6.6
). To this end, we fix velocity fields
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})\in H^{1}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
and consider the convective Cahn–Hilliard system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\phi_{m}+\textnormal{div}(\phi_{m}\mathbf{v}_{\ast})=\Delta\mu_{m}

\displaystyle\text{in}\ Q,

(6.8a)

\displaystyle\mu_{m}=-\Delta\phi_{m}+F^{\prime}(\phi_{m})

\displaystyle\text{in}\ Q,

(6.8b)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\psi_{m}+\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m}\mathbf{w}_{\ast})=\Delta_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}-\beta\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\mu_{m}

\displaystyle\text{on}\ \Sigma,

(6.8c)

\displaystyle\theta_{m}=-\Delta_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}+G^{\prime}(\psi_{m})+\alpha\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi_{m}

\displaystyle\text{on}\ \Sigma,

(6.8d)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}K\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi_{m}=\alpha\psi_{m}-\phi_{m}&\text{if}\ K\in[0,\infty),\\
\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\phi_{m}=0&\text{if}\ K=\infty\end{cases}

\displaystyle\text{on}\ \Sigma,

(6.8e)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}L\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\mu_{m}=\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m}&\text{if}\ L\in(0,\infty),\\
\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\mu_{m}=0&\text{if}\ L=\infty\end{cases}

\displaystyle\text{on}\ \Sigma,

(6.8f)

\displaystyle\phi_{m}|_{t=0}=\phi_{0}

\displaystyle\text{in\penalty\ }\Omega,

(6.8g)

\displaystyle\psi_{m}|_{t=0}=\psi_{0}

\displaystyle\text{on\penalty\ }\Gamma.

(6.8h)

Thanks to
[
54
, Theorem 3.4]
(see also
[
39
, Theorem 3.2]
), there exists a unique solution
(\phi_{m},\psi_{m},\mu_{m},\theta_{m})
to (
6.8
) with

\displaystyle\left\{\;\begin{aligned} (\phi_{m},\psi_{m})&\in H^{1}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\prime})\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K,\alpha})\cap L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{W}^{2,6}),\\
(\mu_{m},\theta_{m})&\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}),\\
(F^{\prime}(\phi_{m}),G^{\prime}(\psi_{m}))&\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{6}),\end{aligned}\right.

(6.9)

in the sense that the variational formulations (
6.4
) and (
6.7
) hold with
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})
instead of
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
. Furthermore, the phase fields
\phi_{m}
and
\psi_{m}
satisfy

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{m}\right|<1\ \text{a.e.\penalty\ in\penalty\ }Q\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\left|\psi_{m}\right|<1\ \text{a.e.\penalty\ on\penalty\ }\Sigma.

(6.10)

We also know from
[
54
, Theorem 3.4]
that
(\phi_{m},\psi_{m},\mu_{m},\theta_{m})
satisfies the energy inequality

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{m}(t),\psi_{m}(t))+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\mu_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad+\chi(L)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s\\
&\quad\leq E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{m}\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(6.11)

for all
t\in[0,T]
, where the bulk-surface free energy is given by

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{free}}(\zeta,\xi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla\zeta\right|^{2}+F(\zeta)\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\right|^{2}+G(\xi)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\chi(K)\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}(\alpha\xi-\zeta)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.

Using Hölder’s and Young’s inequality combined with (
6.10
), we immediately infer from (
6.11
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{m}(t),\psi_{m}(t))+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mu_{m},\theta_{m})\|_{L,\beta}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\end{split}

(6.12)

for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Next, for any
t\in[0,T]
, we make the ansatz

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{v}_{m}(t),\mathbf{w}_{m}(t)\right)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}a_{j}^{k}(t)\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\right)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in\penalty\ }\Omega\times\Gamma

as a solution to the Galerkin approximation of (
6.3
) that reads as

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})(\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla)\mathbf{v}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})(\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\rho^{\prime}\,(\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\nabla)\mathbf{v}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\,(\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}:\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.13)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}\mu_{m}\nabla\phi_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma,

a.e. on
[0,T]
for all
l=1,\ldots,m
, supplemented with the initial condition

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m}\right)|_{t=0}=\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.\penalty\ in\penalty\ }\Omega\times\Gamma.

(6.14)

Setting
\mathbf{A}_{m}(t)=(a_{1}^{m}(t),\ldots,a_{m}^{m}(t))^{T}
, the system (
6.2
) is equivalent to the system of differential equations

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{m}(t)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbf{A}^{m}(t)+\mathbf{L}^{m}(t)\mathbf{A}^{m}(t)=\mathbf{G}^{m}(t),

(6.15)

where

\displaystyle(\mathbf{M}^{m}(t))_{l,j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{L}^{m}(t))_{l,j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})(\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})(\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma})\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{\Omega}\rho^{\prime}\,(\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\,(\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma})\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}:\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{G}^{m}(t))_{l}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\int_{\Omega}\mu_{m}\nabla\phi_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma,

and the initial condition is

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}^{m}(0)_{j}=(\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}),(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}))_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}},\hskip 20.00003ptj=1,\ldots,m.

Proceeding as in
[
40
]
, it is straightforward to check that
\mathbf{M}^{m}(t)
is positive definite for all
t\in[0,T]
.
Defining

\displaystyle f_{m}(t,\mathbf{Y})\coloneqq(\mathbf{M}^{m})^{-1}(t)\mathbf{L}^{m}(t)\mathbf{Y}+(\mathbf{M}^{m})^{-1}(t)\mathbf{G}^{m}(t)

for
\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
t\in[0,T]
, (
6.15
) can be equivalently restated as

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbf{A}^{m}(t)=f_{m}\big(t,\mathbf{A}^{m}(t)\big)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{for\penalty\ }t\in[0,T].

(6.16)

As
(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\in C([0,T];\mathcal{L}^{2})
, we infer that
\mathbf{M}^{m}\in C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{m\times m})
. Since
\mathbf{M}^{m}(t)
is positive definite for all
t\in[0,T]
, we deduce that
\det\big(\mathbf{M}^{m}(\cdot)\big)
is continuous and uniformly positive on
[0,T]
.
Hence, according to Cramer’s rule for the inverse, we also have
(\mathbf{M}^{m})^{-1}\in C([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{m\times m})
. Next, we find functions
h,l\in L^{2}(0,T)
such that

\displaystyle\left|f_{m}(t,\mathbf{Y})\right|\leq h(t)

for
\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, and

\displaystyle\left|f_{m}(t,\mathbf{Y})-f_{m}(t,\overline{\mathbf{Y}})\right|\leq l(t)\left|\mathbf{Y}-\overline{\mathbf{Y}}\right|

for
\mathbf{Y},\overline{\mathbf{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
. Thus, we are in a position to apply Carathéodory’s existence theorem (see, e.g.,
[
78
, Theorem XVIII]
) to find a unique solution
\mathbf{A}^{m}\in H^{1}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{m})
of (
6.16
). This, in turn, implies the existence of a unique pair
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\in H^{1}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
that solves (
6.2
)-(
6.14
).

Now, we multiply (
6.2
) by
a_{l}^{k}
and sum over
l=1,\ldots,m
to find that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho(\phi_{m})\partial_{t}\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma(\psi_{m})\partial_{t}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho^{\prime}\,\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\nabla\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{\prime}\,\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.17)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}\mu_{m}\nabla\phi_{m}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.

First, we notice that

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\rho(\phi_{m}),\partial_{t}\sigma(\psi_{m}))=(\rho^{\prime}\,\partial_{t}\phi_{m},\sigma^{\prime}\,\partial_{t}\psi_{m})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\prime})

which can be easily seen using difference quotients.
In particular, we have

\displaystyle\big<(\rho^{\prime}\,\partial_{t}\phi_{m},\sigma^{\prime}\,\partial_{t}\psi_{m}),(\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2},\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2})\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{\prime}\,\big<\partial_{t}\phi_{m},\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\big>_{H^{1}(\Omega)}+\sigma^{\prime}\,\big<\partial_{t}\psi_{m},\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\big>_{H^{1}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle=\big<(\partial_{t}\phi_{m},\partial_{t}\psi_{m}),(\rho^{\prime}\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2},\sigma^{\prime}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2})\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

Hence, using the weak formulation (
6.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho(\phi_{m})\partial_{t}\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma(\psi_{m})\partial_{t}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\frac{1}{2}\big<(\partial_{t}\phi_{m},\partial_{t}\psi_{m}),(\rho^{\prime}\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2},\sigma^{\prime}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2})\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho^{\prime}\,\phi_{m}\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{\prime}\,\psi_{m}\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho^{\prime}\,\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\nabla\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{\prime}\,\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\chi(L)\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.

Then, since

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=0,

and, as
\rho(\phi_{m})
and
\rho^{\prime}\,\phi_{m}
as well as
\sigma(\psi_{m})
and
\sigma^{\prime}\,\psi_{m}
differ only by an additive constant, respectively, we infer
by means of (
6.2
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)+\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.18)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=-\int_{\Omega}\phi_{m}\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.

Using (
6.10
) and the bulk-surface Korn inequality (see Lemma
5.1
), we can estimate the right-hand side of (
6.2
) as

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\phi_{m}\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\|\nabla\mu_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}{2}\big(\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{C_{K}^{2}}{2\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}\big(\|\nabla\mu_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big).

In this way, we derive the differential inequality

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\rho(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)\\
&\qquad+\frac{\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}{2}\Big(\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\Big)\end{split}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\frac{C_{K}^{2}}{2\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}\big(\|\nabla\mu_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\big).

Integrating over
[0,s]
with
s\in[0,T]
, and using (
6.12
), it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{\rho_{\ast}}{2}\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{\sigma_{\ast}}{2}\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}(s)\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\int_{\Omega}\frac{\rho^{\ast}}{2}\left|\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Omega}(\mathbf{v}_{0})\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{\sigma^{\ast}}{2}\left|\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{w}_{0})\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\frac{C_{K}^{2}}{\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{C_{K}^{2}}{2\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}\int_{0}^{s}\|(\mathbf{v}_{\ast}(\tau),\mathbf{w}_{\ast}(\tau))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau.

This, in turn, entails that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{v}_{m}(s)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\mathbf{w}_{m}(s)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\\
&\quad\leq\rho^{\ast}c_{*}\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\sigma^{\ast}c_{*}\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\frac{2C_{K}^{2}}{\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}c_{*}E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\\
&\qquad+\frac{C_{K}^{2}}{\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}c_{*}\int_{0}^{s}\|(\mathbf{v}_{\ast}(\tau),\mathbf{w}_{\ast}(\tau))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau,\end{split}

(6.19)

where
c_{*}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\rho_{\ast}}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{\ast}}
.
To simplify the presentation, we now define

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\rho^{\ast}c_{*}\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\sigma^{\ast}c_{*}\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\frac{2C_{K}^{2}}{\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}c_{*}E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0}),

\displaystyle C_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{C_{K}^{2}}{\min\{\gamma_{\ast},2\nu_{\ast}\}}c_{*},\hskip 20.00003ptC_{3}\coloneqq C_{1}T.

Assuming

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v}_{\ast}(\tau),\mathbf{w}_{\ast}(\tau))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C_{3}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}t},\hskip 20.00003ptt\in[0,T],

(6.20)

we deduce from (
6.19
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m}(s),\mathbf{w}_{m}(s))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{3}+C_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\|(\mathbf{v}_{\ast}(\tau),\mathbf{w}_{\ast}(\tau))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\;\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{3}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}t}\end{split}

(6.21)

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Furthermore, we also infer from (
6.19
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m}(t),\mathbf{w}_{m}(t))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\leq\big(C_{1}+C_{3}C_{2}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}T}\big)^{1/2}\eqqcolon M_{0}.

(6.22)

Next, we aim to control the time derivative of
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
. To this end, we multiply (
6.2
) by
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}a_{l}^{k}
and sum over
l=1,\ldots,m
. This yields

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\left|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\left|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=-\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})(\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla)\mathbf{v}_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})(\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}:\mathbf{D}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.23)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\rho^{\prime}\,(\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\nabla)\mathbf{v}_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\,(\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{\Omega}\phi_{m}\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma.

Taking advantage of (
6.1
), we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho_{\ast}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\sigma_{\ast}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\\[4.30554pt]
&\quad\leq\rho^{\ast}\|\mathbf{v}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\sigma^{\ast}\|\mathbf{w}_{\ast}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\Gamma)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\\
&\qquad+2\nu^{\ast}\|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\mathbf{D}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+2\nu^{\ast}\|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\\
&\qquad+\gamma^{\ast}\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\left|\rho^{\prime}\,\right|\|\nabla\mu_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\qquad+\left|\sigma^{\prime}\,\right|\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\Gamma)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\nabla\mu_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\qquad+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\\
&\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\chi(L)\left|\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime}\,\right|\|\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{\infty}(\Gamma)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\\[4.30554pt]
&\quad\leq C(\rho^{\ast}+\sigma^{\ast})\|(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{W}^{2,4}}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\nu^{\ast}C_{m}^{2}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}+\gamma^{\ast}\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\\
&\qquad+C\big(\left|\rho^{\prime}\,\right|+\left|\sigma^{\prime}\,\right|\big)\|(\nabla\mu_{m},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{W}^{2,4}}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+C\|(\nabla\mu_{m},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\chi(L)C\left|\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime}\,\right|\|\beta\theta-\mu_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\\[4.30554pt]
&\quad\leq CC_{m}(\rho^{\ast}+\sigma^{\ast})\|(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\nu^{\ast}C_{m}^{2}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}+\gamma^{\ast}\|\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\\
&\qquad+CC_{m}\big(\left|\rho^{\prime}\,\right|+\left|\sigma^{\prime}\,\right|\big)\|(\nabla\mu_{m},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+C\|(\nabla\mu_{m},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\\
&\qquad+\chi(L)CC_{m}\left|\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime}\,\right|\|\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}.\\
\end{split}

(6.24)

Eventually, in view of (
6.11
) and (
6.20
)-(
6.22
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{m}(\tau),\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{m}(\tau))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq 3\Big(CC_{m}(\rho^{\ast}+\sigma^{\ast})c_{*}M_{0}\Big)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+3\Big(\nu^{\ast}C_{m}^{2}c_{*}\Big)^{2}C_{3}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}T}+3\Big(\gamma^{\ast}c_{*}\Big)^{2}C_{3}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}T}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+3\Big(CC_{m}\Big(\left|\rho^{\prime}\,\right|+\left|\sigma^{\prime}\,\right|\Big)c_{*}M_{0}+M_{0}\Big)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|(\nabla\mu_{m}(\tau),\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}(\tau))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\Big(CC_{m}\left|\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime}\,\right|c_{*}M_{0}\Big)^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\beta\theta_{m}(\tau)-\mu_{m}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq\Bigg(3\Big(CC_{m}(\rho^{\ast}+\sigma^{\ast})c_{*}M_{0}\Big)^{2}+3\Big(\nu^{\ast}C_{m}^{2}c_{*}\Big)^{2}+3\Big(\gamma^{\ast}c_{*}\Big)\Bigg)C_{3}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}T}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+3\Big(CC_{m}\Big(\left|\rho^{\prime}\,\right|+\left|\sigma^{\prime}\,\right|\Big)c_{*}M_{0}+M_{0}\Big)^{2}\Big(2E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})+C_{3}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}T}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\Big(CC_{m}\left|\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime}\,\right|c_{*}M_{0}\Big)^{2}\Big(2E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})+C_{3}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}T}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\eqqcolon M_{m}^{2},

(6.25)

where
M_{m}
depends only on
\rho_{\ast},\rho^{\ast},\sigma_{\ast},\sigma^{\ast},\gamma^{\ast},E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0}),L,T,\Omega
and
m
.

Now, we introduce the setting of the fixed-point argument. To this end, we define the set

\displaystyle\mathbfcal{M}=\left\{(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in H^{1}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})\;\middle|\;\begin{aligned} &\int_{0}^{t}\|(\mathbf{v}(\tau),\mathbf{w}(\tau))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C_{3}\mathbf{e}^{C_{2}t},\ t\in[0,T],\\
&\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{v},\partial_{t}\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})}\leq M_{m}\end{aligned}\right\},

which is a subset of
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
. Next, we define the map

\displaystyle\Lambda:\mathbfcal{M}\rightarrow L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m}),\hskip 20.00003pt\Lambda(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})=(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m}),

where
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
is the solution to system (
6.2
) in which the functions
(\phi_{m},\psi_{m},\mu_{m},\theta_{m})
are the solution of (
6.8
). In light of (
6.21
) and (
6.2
), we infer that
\Lambda(\mathbfcal{M})\subseteq\mathbfcal{M}
. It is easily seen that
\mathbfcal{M}
is convex and closed. Furthermore,
\mathbfcal{M}
is a compact set in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
.

We are left to prove that the map
\Lambda
is continuous. To this end, let
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{\ast},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{\ast})\in\mathbfcal{M}
be arbitrary and let
\{(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbfcal{M}
be a sequence with
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n})\rightarrow(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{\ast},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{\ast})
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
. Arguing as above, we find a corresponding sequence
\{(\widehat{\phi}_{n},\widehat{\psi}_{n},\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\theta}_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and a quadruplet
(\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{\ast},\widehat{\mu}_{\ast},\widehat{\theta}_{\ast})
, which solve the convective bulk-surface Cahn–Hilliard equation (
6.8
) with
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n})
and
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{\ast},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{\ast})
instead of
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})
, respectively. Then, since
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{\ast},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{\ast})
belongs to
\mathbfcal{M}
,
[
39
, Theorem 3.5]
provides the continuous dependence estimate

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|((\widehat{\phi}_{n}(t)-\widehat{\phi}(t),\widehat{\psi}_{n}(t)-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast}(t))\|_{L,\beta,\ast}^{2}+\|(\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\leq C\|(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n}-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{\ast},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n}-\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{\ast})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\|(\widehat{\phi}_{n}(t)-\widehat{\phi}(t),\widehat{\psi}_{n}(t)-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast}(t))\|_{L,\beta,\ast}^{2}.

Also, by this construction, we have
(\widehat{\phi}_{n}(0),\widehat{\psi}_{n}(0))=(\widehat{\phi}_{\ast}(0),\widehat{\psi}_{\ast}(0))=(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})
.
Hence, if
K\in[0,\infty)
, the bulk-surface Poincaré inequality and Gronwall’s lemma imply that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|(\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\prime})\cap L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})}^{2}\\
&\qquad\leq\mathbf{e}^{CT}\int_{0}^{T}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n}(\tau)-\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{\ast}(\tau),\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n}(\tau)-\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{\ast}(\tau))\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\tau\rightarrow 0\qquad\text{as\penalty\ }n\rightarrow\infty.\end{split}

(6.26)

If
K=\infty
, we employ Ehrling’s lemma to deduce that

\displaystyle\|(\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|(\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\|(\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\|_{L,\beta,\ast}^{2}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{2}&\leq\|(\nabla\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\nabla\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}^{2}\\
&\qquad+C\|(\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\|_{L,\beta,\ast}^{2}\,.\end{split}

Arguing as in the case
K\in[0,\infty)
, we conclude that (
6.26
) remains true for
K=\infty
.
Hence, for any choice of
K\in[0,\infty]
, we conclude that

\displaystyle(\widehat{\phi}_{n},\widehat{\psi}_{n})\to(\widehat{\phi}_{\ast},\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{strongly in $L^{\infty}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\prime})\cap L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})$ and a.e.\penalty\ in $Q\times\Sigma$,}

(6.27)

as
n\to\infty
, up to extraction of a subsequence.

As
\{(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
lies in
\mathbfcal{M}
, it is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
.
Therefore, proceeding as in the proof of
[
54
, Theorem 3.4]
(see also
[
39
]
), we deduce the uniform estimate

\displaystyle\|(F_{1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\phi}_{n}),G_{1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\psi}_{n}))\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{2})}+\|(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\theta}_{n})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})}\leq C.

(6.28)

Consequently, there exists a pair
(F_{\ast},G_{\ast})\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{2})
such that, up to subsequence extraction,

\displaystyle(F_{1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\phi}_{n}),G_{1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\psi}_{n}))\rightarrow(F_{\ast},G_{\ast})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{2})

(6.29)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. To identify the limit functions
(F_{\ast},G_{\ast})
with
(F_{1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\phi}_{\ast}),G_{1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\psi}_{\ast}))
, we employ Minty’s trick. To be precise, from the weak-strong convergence principle, we infer

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}F_{1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\phi}_{n})\widehat{\phi}_{n}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}F_{\ast}\widehat{\phi}_{\ast}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t,

(6.30)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}G_{1}^{\prime}(\widehat{\psi}_{n})\widehat{\psi}_{n}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}G_{\ast}\widehat{\psi}_{\ast}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t,

(6.31)

from which the claim readily follows due to the assumptions on
F_{1}
and
G_{1}
(see, e.g.,
[
13
, Lemma 1.3, p. 42]
).
Combing (
6.27
) and (
6.29
), we conclude from the weak formulation (
6.7
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\widehat{\mu}_{\ast})\eta\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\widehat{\theta}_{\ast})\vartheta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(\nabla\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\nabla\widehat{\phi}_{\ast})\cdot\nabla\eta+(F^{\prime}(\widehat{\phi}_{n})-F^{\prime}(\widehat{\phi}_{\ast}))\eta\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\vartheta+(G^{\prime}(\widehat{\psi}_{n})-G^{\prime}(\widehat{\psi}_{\ast}))\vartheta\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\end{split}

(6.32)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\chi(K)\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\alpha(\widehat{\psi}_{n}-\widehat{\psi}_{\ast})-(\widehat{\phi}_{n}-\widehat{\phi}_{\ast}))(\alpha\vartheta-\eta)\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\longrightarrow 0

as
n\rightarrow\infty
for all
(\eta,\vartheta)\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K,\alpha})
. This proves that

\displaystyle(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\theta}_{n})\rightarrow(\widehat{\mu}_{\ast},\widehat{\theta}_{\ast})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K,\alpha})^{\prime}).

(6.33)

In combination with (
6.28
) we infer that, up to subsequence extraction,

\displaystyle(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\theta}_{n})\rightarrow(\widehat{\mu}_{\ast},\widehat{\theta}_{\ast})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}).

(6.34)

As the limit functions in (
6.27
), (
6.29
), and (
6.34
) do not depend on the choice of the extracted subsequence, we conclude that these convergences remain true for the whole sequence.

Now, to simplify the notation, we set
(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n})=\Lambda(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n})
. Then, as
(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n})\in\mathbfcal{M}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we infer the existence of
(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast})\in H^{1}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
such that, up to subsequence extraction,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in $H^{1}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})$}.

(6.35)

As
\mathbfcal{V}_{m}
is finite-dimensional, the embedding
H^{1}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})\hookrightarrow C([0,T];\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
is compact. Together with the first inverse Sobolev embedding from (
6.1
), we infer that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast}),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }C([0,T];\mathbfcal{V}_{m}),

(6.36)

\displaystyle(\nabla\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n},\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\nabla\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }C([0,T];\mathbfcal{L}^{2}),

(6.37)

up to subsequence extraction.
Moreover, as the set
\mathbfcal{M}\subset L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
is bounded, closed, and convex, it is weakly sequentially compact. This readily yields
(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast})\in\mathbfcal{M}
.
By the definition of
\Lambda
, we know that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho(\widehat{\phi}_{n})\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\widehat{\psi}_{n})\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\rho(\widehat{\phi}_{n})(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n}\cdot\nabla)\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\widehat{\psi}_{n})(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma})\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}\rho^{\prime}\,(\nabla\widehat{\mu}_{n}\cdot\nabla)\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma^{\prime}\,(\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\theta}_{n}\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma})\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.38)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\widehat{\phi}_{n})\mathbf{D}\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n}:\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\widehat{\psi}_{n})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\widehat{\phi}_{n},\widehat{\psi}_{n})\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}\widehat{\mu}_{n}\nabla\widehat{\phi}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\widehat{\theta}_{n}\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\psi}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}-\rho^{\prime})(\beta\widehat{\theta}_{n}-\widehat{\mu}_{n})\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{l}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma,

for all
l=1,\ldots,m
. Due to the convergences (
6.27
), (
6.34
), (
6.36
) and (
6.37
), it is straightforward to pass to the limit
n\to\infty
in all terms.
Furthermore, convergence (
6.36
) implies that the initial condition
(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast})|_{t=0}=\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
a.e. in
\Omega\times\Gamma
is fulfilled.
In this way, we infer that
(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast})
is a solution to system (
6.2
)-(
6.14
). As the solution of system (
6.2
)-(
6.14
) is unique, this readily implies
(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast})=\Lambda(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{\ast},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{\ast})
. Consequently, using convergence (
6.36
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\Lambda(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{n},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{n})=(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{n},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{n})\rightarrow(\mathbf{u}^{\Omega}_{\ast},\mathbf{u}^{\Gamma}_{\ast})=\Lambda(\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{\ast},\widehat{\mathbf{w}}_{\ast})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m}).

(6.39)

This proves that the map
\Lambda
is continuous.

Finally, we are in a position to apply Schauder’s fixed-point theorem, which implies that the map
\Lambda
has a fixed-point in
\mathbfcal{M}
. Hence, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, this ensures the existence of an approximate solution
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m},\phi_{m},\psi_{m},\mu_{m},\theta_{m})
to the discrete problem (
6.3
)-(
6.6
).

6.3  Uniform estimates and passage to the limit

Now, we establish bounds on the approximate solutions
(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m},\phi_{m},\psi_{m},\mu_{m},\theta_{m})
which are uniform with respect to
m\in\mathbb{N}
. We start by taking
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})=(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})
in (
6.3
). Integrating by parts yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\rho(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{2}\sigma(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma+\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=-\int_{\Omega}\phi_{m}\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.40)

(cf. (
6.2
)).
Next, from (
6.11
), we already know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{m}(t),\psi_{m}(t))+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\mu_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s\\
&\qquad+\chi(L)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s\\
&\quad\leq E_{\mathrm{free}}(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{m}\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(6.41)

Thus, as
E_{\mathrm{tot}}=E_{\mathrm{kin}}+E_{\mathrm{free}}
, integrating (
6.3
) in time over
[0,t]
for
0<t\leq T
, and summing with (
6.41
), we obtain

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{tot}}(\mathbf{v}_{m}(t),\mathbf{w}_{m}(t),\phi_{m}(t),\psi_{m}(t))

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\mu_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s

(6.42)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\chi(L)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\leq E_{\mathrm{tot}}(\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}),\phi_{0},\psi_{0})

for all
t\in[0,T]
. By the assumptions on the initial data
(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})
and the properties of the projection
\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}
, we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{\mathrm{tot}}(\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}),\phi_{0},\psi_{0})&\leq\frac{\rho^{\ast}+\sigma^{\ast}}{2}\|(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\|_{\mathbfcal{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\|_{K,\alpha}^{2}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\Omega}F(\phi_{0})\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}G(\psi_{0})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\\
&\leq C.\end{split}

(6.43)

Thus, using Lemma
5.1
and the bounds
\left|\phi_{m}\right|<1
a.e. in
Q
and
\left|\psi_{m}\right|<1
a.e. on
\Sigma
, we readily deduce from (
6.3
) that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}+\|(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1})}\leq C,

(6.44)

\displaystyle\|(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})}\leq C,

(6.45)

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\mu_{m},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}+\chi(L)^{1/2}\|\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C.

(6.46)

This allows us to proceed as in the proof of
[
54
, Theorem 3.4]
(see also
[
39
]
) to derive the uniform estimate

\displaystyle\|(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\|_{H^{1}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\prime})}+\|(F_{1}^{\prime}(\phi_{m}),G_{1}^{\prime}(\psi_{m}))\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{2})}+\|(\mu_{m},\theta_{m})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})}\leq C.

(6.47)

Consequently, based on the uniform estimates (
6.44
)-(
6.47
), the Banach–Alaoglu theorem and the Aubin–Lions–Simon lemma imply the existence of a sextuplet
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w},\phi,\psi,\mu,\theta)
such that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}}),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly-star in\penalty\ }L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}),

(6.48)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\phi_{m},\partial_{t}\psi_{m})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\partial_{t}\phi,\partial_{t}\psi)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\prime}),

(6.49)

\displaystyle(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly-star in\penalty\ }L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K,\alpha}),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{s})\ \text{for any\penalty\ }s\in[0,1),

(6.50)

\displaystyle(\mu_{m},\theta_{m})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mu,\theta)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}),

(6.51)

\displaystyle\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\beta\theta-\mu

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))

(6.52)

along a subsequence
m\rightarrow\infty
. Additionally, in view of (
6.47
), we obtain

\displaystyle(F^{\prime}(\phi_{m}),G^{\prime}(\psi_{m}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow(F^{\prime}(\phi),G^{\prime}(\psi))

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{2}),

(6.53)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Here, we made use of the fact that
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
G_{1}^{\prime}
are maximal monotone operators to identify the limit functions (cf. Subsection
6.2
or
[
54
]
for more details). From (
6.53
) we immediately deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\phi\right|<1\hskip 10.00002pt\text{a.e.\penalty\ in\penalty\ }Q\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\left|\psi\right|<1\hskip 10.00002pt\text{a.e.\penalty\ on\penalty\ }\Sigma.

(6.54)

In view of these convergences, it is straightforward to pass to the limit
m\rightarrow\infty
in the weak formulations (
6.4
) and (
6.7
). This shows that the weak formulations (
4.7b
) and (
4.7
) are fulfilled.

To verify the initial condition
(\phi,\psi)|_{t=0}=(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})
a.e. in
\Omega\times\Gamma
, we simply notice that

\displaystyle(\phi_{m}(0),\psi_{m}(0))=(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{a.e.\penalty\ in\penalty\ }\Omega\times\Gamma

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
as well as

\displaystyle(\phi_{m}(0),\psi_{m}(0))\rightarrow(\phi(0),\psi(0))\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }\mathcal{L}^{2}

as
m\rightarrow\infty
because of (
6.50
).

Next, to show that (
4.7
) holds, we rewrite (
6.3
) again in the conservative form (see (
1.15
)) and integrate with respect to time. We get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{m}:\nabla\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{w}_{m}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}:\mathbf{D}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{v}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{J}_{m}:\nabla\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{K}_{m}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu_{m}\nabla\phi_{m}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}+\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\end{split}

(6.55)

for all
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{V}_{m})
, where

(\mathbf{J}_{m},\mathbf{K}_{m})=(-\rho^{\prime}\,\nabla\mu_{m},-\sigma^{\prime}\,\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m})
.
Now, proceeding similarly to the line of argument in
[
2
, Section 5]
), we use the uniform estimates (
6.44
)-(
6.47
) along with standard interpolation results to show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{m},\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{w}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}&\ \text{\penalty\ is uniformly bounded in\penalty\ }\ L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{3/2}),\\
\{(\mathbf{v}_{m}\otimes\nabla\mu_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m}\otimes\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}&\ \text{\penalty\ is uniformly bounded in\penalty\ }\ L^{8/7}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{4/3}),\\
\{(\mu_{m}\nabla\phi_{m},\theta_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}&\ \text{\penalty\ is uniformly bounded in\penalty\ }\ L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{3/2}),\\
\{(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}&\ \text{\penalty\ is uniformly bounded in\penalty\ }\ L^{8/7}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{4/3}(\Gamma)).\end{split}

(6.56)

Moreover, via the trace embedding
H^{1}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Gamma)
, we infer from (
6.44
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}&\ \text{\penalty\ is uniformly bounded in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}),\\
\{\mathbf{w}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}&\ \text{\penalty\ is uniformly bounded in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)).\end{split}

(6.57)

In particular, this means that all terms appearing in (
6.56
) and (
6.57
) are uniformly bounded in
L^{8/7}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{4/3})
or
L^{8/7}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{4/3}(\Gamma))
, respectively.

Let now
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in L^{8}(0,T;\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})
be arbitrary. Then
\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})
is an admissible test function in (
6.55
). Hence, by a comparison argument, we infer that
\{\partial_{t}\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m},\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{8/7}(0,T;(\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})^{\prime})
. In addition, we know that
\{\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m},\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}})
. Therefore, according to the Aubin–Lions lemma, there exists
(\mathbf{v}^{\star},\mathbf{w}^{\star})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
such that

\displaystyle\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m},\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow(\mathbf{v}^{\star},\mathbf{w}^{\star})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}),

(6.58)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
, along a non-relabeled subsequence.
In order to show that
(\mathbf{v}^{\star},\mathbf{w}^{\star})=\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v},\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w}))
, we first establish the convergence

\displaystyle(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m},\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow(\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v},\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})

(6.59)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. To this end, let

(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
be arbitrary.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m}-\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}-\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(\rho(\phi_{m})-\rho(\phi))\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi)(\mathbf{v}_{m}-\mathbf{v})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi_{m})-\sigma(\psi))\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi)(\mathbf{w}_{m}-\mathbf{w})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\[4.30554pt]
&\quad\longrightarrow 0\end{split}

(6.60)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Here, we have used the strong convergence
((\rho(\phi_{m})-\rho(\phi))\widehat{\mathbf{v}},(\sigma(\psi_{m})-\sigma(\psi))\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\rightarrow 0
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
as
m\rightarrow\infty
as well as the weak convergence (
6.3
).
This means that (
6.59
) is established.
For any
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
, we now use the convergence (
6.59
) along with the strong convergence
\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\rightarrow\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
(see Corollary
5.10
)
to deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{v}^{\star}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}^{\star}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Omega}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\Big)\\
&\qquad=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Omega}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}})\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\Big)\\
&\qquad=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}})\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}(\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w})\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t.\end{split}

(6.61)

This proves that
(\mathbf{v}^{\star},\mathbf{w}^{\star})=\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v},\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w}))
.

Next, due to (
6.3
) and (
6.58
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\left|\mathbf{v}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\left|\mathbf{w}_{m}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Omega}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\cdot\mathbf{w}_{m}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad\longrightarrow\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}(\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v})\cdot\mathbf{v}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w})\cdot\mathbf{w}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\end{split}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi)\left|\mathbf{v}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi)\left|\mathbf{w}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t,

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, arguing analogously to (
6.59
), we conclude that

\displaystyle(\rho(\phi_{m})^{1/2}\mathbf{v}_{m},\sigma(\psi_{m})^{1/2}\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow(\rho(\phi)^{1/2}\mathbf{v},\sigma(\psi)^{1/2}\mathbf{w})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}).

Lastly, since
\rho(\phi_{m})\rightarrow\rho(\phi)
almost everywhere in
Q
, and
\rho\geq\rho_{\ast}>0
, we use Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem to deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{m}=\rho(\phi_{m})^{-1/2}(\rho(\phi_{m})^{1/2}\mathbf{v}_{m})\rightarrow\mathbf{v}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega))

(6.62)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. In a similar manner, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}_{m}=\sigma(\psi_{m})^{-1/2}(\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow\mathbf{w}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma))

(6.63)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
.

Finally, in order to recover the weak formulation (
4.7
),
let
\pi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(0,T)
be arbitrary, and for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
, let
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})=(\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})
. Then, for
m\geq j
, we infer from (
6.55
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m})\cdot\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\cdot\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{m}:\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{w}_{m}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}:\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{v}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{J}_{m}:\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t

(6.64)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{K}_{m}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu_{m}\nabla\phi_{m}:\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}+\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t.

For the first two terms, we use integration by parts together with (
6.59
) to deduce

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m})\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}(\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j})\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\partial_{t}(\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\rightarrow-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v}\cdot\partial_{t}(\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j})\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w}\cdot\partial_{t}(\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

as
m\rightarrow\infty
.

Moreover, recalling the boundedness of
\{(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{6})
, the continuous embedding
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{s})
for
s\in[1,2]
, and the strong convergences (
6.62
) and (
6.63
), we infer via an interpolation argument that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{6-r})

as
m\rightarrow\infty
for all
0<r\leq 5
.
Furthermore, since
\{(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is also bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
, an interpolation argument reveals that for any
1\leq q<\infty
, there exists
\tau=\tau(q,r)>0
such that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{q}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2+\tau})

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. To be precise, it holds that

\displaystyle 2+\tau=\frac{2(6-r)}{\frac{2}{q}(r-4)+6-r}.

In particular, choosing
r=3
yields

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{3})

(6.65)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Then, for
q=4
, we obtain
2+\tau=12/5
, and hence,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{4}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{12/5})

(6.66)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
.
Next, we recall that for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
, the eigenfunction
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})
belongs to
\mathbfcal{H}^{2}\hookrightarrow\mathbfcal{L}^{d+1}
(cf. Theorem
5.9
). Hence, because of Theorem
5.7
, we even have
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})\in\mathbfcal{W}^{2,d+1}\hookrightarrow\mathbfcal{W}^{1,\infty}
.
This allows us to use Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem to obtain the convergence

\displaystyle\big(\rho(\phi_{m})\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi,\sigma(\psi_{m})\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\big)\rightarrow\big(\rho(\phi)\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi,\sigma(\psi)\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\big)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in \penalty\ }L^{4}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{12})

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently, in combination with (
6.3
) and (
6.66
), we obtain

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{m}:\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{w}_{m}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\rightarrow-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v}:\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w}\otimes\mathbf{w}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Next, we employ the continuity and boundedness of
\nu_{\Omega},\nu_{\Gamma}
(see
(A4)
) together with Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem to infer

\displaystyle(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi,2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi)\rightarrow(2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi,2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi)

strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
as
m\rightarrow\infty
. As
(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m},\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m})\rightarrow(\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v},\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w})
weakly in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
as
m\rightarrow\infty
, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{m})\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{m}:\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{m})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\rightarrow\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}:\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Furthermore, arguing as above, we find that
\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\rightarrow\gamma(\phi,\psi)\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi
strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma))
as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, since
\mathbf{w}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbf{w}
strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma))
as
m\rightarrow\infty
, this yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi_{m},\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\rightarrow\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Next, due to (
6.51
), we find that
(\mathbf{J}_{m},\mathbf{K}_{m})\rightarrow(\mathbf{J},\mathbf{K})
weakly in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
as
m\rightarrow\infty
, where
(\mathbf{J},\mathbf{K})=(-\rho^{\prime}\,\nabla\mu,-\sigma^{\prime}\,\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta)
.
Thus, using (
6.62
)-(
6.63
) along with the regularity
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})\in\mathbfcal{W}^{1,\infty}
that was already shown above, we infer that

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{v}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{J}_{m}:\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{m}\otimes\mathbf{K}_{m}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\rightarrow-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{J}:\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}\otimes\mathbf{K}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

as
m\rightarrow\infty
.
For the first two terms on the right-hand side of (
6.3
), we deduce that

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\phi_{m}\nabla\mu_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\psi_{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\rightarrow-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\phi\nabla\mu\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\psi\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

as
m\rightarrow\infty
, due to the convergences (
6.50
) and (
6.51
). Finally, for the last term on the right-hand side of (
6.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}+\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\rightarrow\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}+\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta-\mu)\mathbf{w}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

as
m\rightarrow\infty
, due to the convergences (
6.52
) and (
6.63
).

Altogether, we can now pass to the limit
m\rightarrow\infty
in (
6.3
). In this way, we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v}\cdot\partial_{t}(\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j})\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w}\cdot\partial_{t}(\pi\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v}:\nabla\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w}\otimes\mathbf{w}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi)\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}:\mathbf{D}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi)\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\phi,\psi)\mathbf{w}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{J}:\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}\otimes\mathbf{K}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu\nabla\phi\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\theta\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+\frac{\chi(L)}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\beta\sigma^{\prime}+\rho^{\prime})(\beta\theta_{m}-\mu_{m})\mathbf{w}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\pi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
\pi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,T)
.
Since
\,\mathrm{span}\,\{(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j}):j\in\mathbb{N}\}
is dense in
\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}}
(cf. Corollary
5.11
), we know that

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{N}\pi_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{j},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{j})\;\middle|\;N\in\mathbb{N},\,\pi_{j}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,T)\;\text{for}\;j=1,...,N\right\}\subset C_{c}^{\infty}((0,T);\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}})

is dense. Consequently, the sextuplet
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w},\phi,\psi,\mu,\theta)
satisfies the weak formulation (
4.7
)-(
4.7
).

6.4  Verification of the initial condition for
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})

In this step, we show that the initial conditions for
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
are fulfilled. To this end, we start with the following lemma, which has been adapted from
[
2
, Section 5.2]
to the current setting.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}),(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})\in\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}}
and
(\overline{\rho},\overline{\sigma})\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}
with
\overline{\rho}\geq\overline{\rho}_{0}>0
and
\overline{\sigma}\geq\overline{\sigma}_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\overline{\rho}\,\mathbf{v}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\sigma}\,\mathbf{w}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=\int_{\Omega}\overline{\rho}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\sigma}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.67)

for all
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}}
. Then it holds
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})=(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})
a.e. in
\Omega\times\Gamma
.

According to Corollary
5.12
, there exists a sequence
\{(\mathbf{v}_{N},\mathbf{w}_{N})\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}}
such that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{N},\mathbf{w}_{N})\to(\mathbf{v}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{w}-\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})\hskip 10.00002pt\text{strongly in $\mathbfcal{L}^{2}$}

as
N\to\infty
. We now test the difference of (
6.67
) written for
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
and (
6.67
) written for
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})
with
(\mathbf{v}_{N},\mathbf{w}_{N})
. Passing to the limit
N\to\infty
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\overline{\rho}\left|\mathbf{v}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\sigma}\left|\mathbf{w}-\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=0.

Since
\overline{\rho}
and
\overline{\sigma}
are uniformly positive, we infer
\mathbf{v}=\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}
in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
and
\mathbf{w}=\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}
in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)
. This proves the claim.
∎

At first, we show the weak continuity in time for the projection of
(\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v},\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w})
onto the divergence-free vector fields. To this end, we consider
(\mathbf{v}_{m}^{\star},\mathbf{w}_{m}^{\star})=\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\rho_{m}(\phi_{m})\mathbf{v}_{m},\sigma_{m}(\psi_{m})\mathbf{w}_{m})
, and deduce from the arguments above that

\displaystyle\{(\mathbf{v}_{m}^{\star},\mathbf{w}_{m}^{\star})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\text{is bounded in}\hskip 10.00002ptW^{1,8/7}(0,T;(\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})^{\prime})\hookrightarrow C([0,T];(\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})^{\prime}),

(6.68)

\displaystyle\{(\mathbf{v}_{m}^{\star},\mathbf{w}_{m}^{\star})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\text{is bounded in}\hskip 10.00002ptL^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}).

(6.69)

In view of these bounds, we already showed in (
6.58
) that
(\mathbf{v}_{m}^{\star},\mathbf{w}_{m}^{\star})\rightarrow(\mathbf{v}^{\star},\mathbf{w}^{\star})
strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
as
m\rightarrow\infty
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\mathbf{v}_{m}^{\star},\mathbf{w}_{m}^{\star})\to(\mathbf{v}^{\star},\mathbf{w}^{\star})&\quad\text{weakly in $W^{1,8/7}(0,T;(\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})^{\prime})$}\\
&\quad\text{and weakly-star in $L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})$}\end{split}

(6.70)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. These convergences are first obtained after subsequence extraction, but as the limit is independent of the extracted subsequence, the convergences hold for the whole sequence.
In particular, using Lemma
2.1
, we infer that
(\mathbf{v}^{\star},\mathbf{w}^{\star})\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
.
Moreover, in view of the continuous embedding stated in (
6.68
), we infer that the evaluation operator

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr_{0}}:W^{1,8/7}(0,T;(\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})^{\prime})\to(\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})^{\prime},\hskip 10.00002pt(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})\mapsto\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}(0),\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}(0)\big)

is continuous. Consequently, in combination with the weak convergence (
6.70
), we obtain

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{v}_{m}^{\star}(0),\mathbf{w}_{m}^{\star}(0)\big)\to\big(\mathbf{v}^{\star}(0),\mathbf{w}^{\star}(0)\big)\hskip 10.00002pt\text{weakly in $(\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})^{\prime}$}

(6.71)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
.

Now, for any
t\in[0,T]
, we consider the auxiliary problems

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}\left(\frac{1}{\rho(\phi(t))}\nabla p(t)\right)

\displaystyle=\textnormal{div}\left(\frac{1}{\rho(\phi(t))}\mathbf{v}^{\star}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in\penalty\ }\Omega,

(6.72a)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}p(t)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on\penalty\ }\Gamma,

(6.72b)

and

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma(\psi(t))}\nabla_{\Gamma}q(t)\right)=\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma(\psi(t))}\mathbf{w}^{\star}(t)\right)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on\penalty\ }\Gamma.

(6.73)

The corresponding weak formulations read, for all
t\in[0,T]
, as

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\rho(\phi(t))}\nabla p(t)\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\rho(\phi(t))}\mathbf{v}^{\star}(t)\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x

(6.74)

for all
\zeta\in H^{1}(\Omega)
and

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sigma(\psi(t))}\nabla_{\Gamma}q(t)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=-\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sigma(\psi(t))}\mathbf{w}^{\star}(t)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

(6.75)

for all
\xi\in H^{1}(\Gamma)
, respectively. By the Lax–Milgram theorem, we find unique weak solutions
p\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Omega))
and
q\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Gamma))
, which satisfy

\displaystyle\|\nabla p(t)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\mathbf{v}^{\star}(t)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

(6.76)

and

\displaystyle\|\nabla q(t)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{w}^{\star}(t)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}

(6.77)

with a constant
C>0
independent of
t
, respectively. Here, we used the fact that both
\rho\in L^{\infty}(Q)
and
\sigma\in L^{\infty}(\Sigma)
are bounded from above and from below by positive constants.

We now claim that
(\nabla p,\nabla_{\Gamma}q)\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
. As the equations for
p
and
q
are completely uncoupled, we only prove that
\nabla p\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega))
, while the other assertion follows by similar arguments. To prove the claim, let
t\in[0,T]
be arbitrary, and let
\{t_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be an arbitrary sequence with
t_{n}\rightarrow t
as
n\rightarrow\infty
