. From
\mathbf{v}^{\star}\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega))
we infer in particular that
\{\mathbf{v}^{\star}(t_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
. Thus, due to (
6.76
), also
\{\nabla p(t_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
.
Since
\{\nabla\tilde{p}:\tilde{p}\in H^{1}(\Omega)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Omega)\}
is a closed subspace of
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, we infer that there exists
\tilde{p}\in H^{1}(\Omega)\cap L^{2}_{(0)}(\Omega)
such that, up to subsequence extraction,

\nabla p(t_{n})\rightarrow\nabla\tilde{p}
weakly in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
By passing to the limit in the weak formulation (
6.76
) written at
t_{n}
, we notice that
\tilde{p}
is a weak solution to (
6.74
). Because of uniqueness, this readily yields
\nabla\tilde{p}=\nabla p(t)
. In particular, this means that the weak limit is independent of the extracted subsequence. Thus, the convergence
\nabla p(t_{n})\rightarrow\nabla\tilde{p}
weakly in
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
, remains true for the whole sequence.
This shows that
\nabla p\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega))
. The statement
\nabla_{\Gamma}q\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma))
can be verified analogously. Thus, the regularity
(\nabla p,\nabla_{\Gamma}q)\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
is established.

As seen before, we know that
(\mathbf{v}^{\star},\mathbf{w}^{\star})=\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\rho(\phi)\mathbf{v},\sigma(\psi)\mathbf{w})
. In particular, it holds

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{v}^{\star}(t),\mathbf{w}^{\star}(t)\big)=\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}\big(\rho(\phi(t))\mathbf{v}(t),\sigma(\psi(t))\mathbf{w}(t)\big)\hskip 20.00003pt\text{almost everywhere in\penalty\ }(0,T).

(6.78)

By the characterization of the projection
\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}=(\mathbf{P}^{\Omega}_{\textnormal{div}},\mathbf{P}^{\Gamma}_{\textnormal{div}}):\mathbfcal{L}^{2}\to\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}
, the last identity entails that

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{v}^{\star}(t),\mathbf{w}^{\star}(t)\big)=\big(\rho(\phi(t))\mathbf{v}(t)-\nabla\hat{p}(t),\sigma(\psi(t))\mathbf{w}(t)-\nabla_{\Gamma}\hat{q}(t)\big)

(6.79)

almost everywhere in
(0,T)
, where
\hat{p}(t)
can be fixed as the weak solution to the Poisson–Neumann problem

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{p}(t)

\displaystyle=\textnormal{div}\big(\rho(\phi(t))\mathbf{v}(t)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{in\penalty\ }\Omega,

(6.80)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{n}}\hat{p}(t)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on\penalty\ }\Gamma,

(6.81)

whereas
\hat{q}(t)
can be fixed as the weak solution of the inhomogeneous Laplace–Beltrami equation

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Gamma}\hat{q}(t)=\textnormal{div}_{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi(t))\mathbf{w}(t))\hskip 20.00003pt\text{on\penalty\ }\Gamma.

(6.82)

Dividing the first component and second component in (
6.79
) by
\rho(\phi(t))
and
\sigma(\psi(t))
, respectively, we infer

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho(\phi(t))}\mathbf{v}^{\star}(t)+\frac{1}{\rho(\phi(t))}\nabla\hat{p}(t),

(6.83)

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma(\psi(t))}\mathbf{w}^{\star}(t)+\frac{1}{\sigma(\psi(t))}\nabla_{\Gamma}\hat{q}(t).

(6.84)

Hence, for almost all
t\in(0,T)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\rho(\phi(t))}\nabla\hat{p}(t)\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\rho(\phi(t))}\mathbf{v}^{\star}(t)\cdot\nabla\zeta\;\mathrm{d}x

for all
\zeta\in H^{1}(\Omega)
, and

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sigma(\psi(t))}\nabla_{\Gamma}\hat{q}(t)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma=-\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sigma(\psi(t))}\mathbf{w}^{\star}(t)\cdot\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

for all
\xi\in H^{1}(\Gamma)
. This means that
\hat{p}
and
\hat{q}
are weak solutions of (
6.72
) and (
6.73
), respectively. Due to the uniqueness of those weak solutions, we infer
\nabla\hat{p}(t)=\nabla p(t)
as well as
\nabla_{\Gamma}\hat{q}(t)=\nabla_{\Gamma}q(t)
for almost every
t\in(0,T)
. Thus, by redefinition of
(\nabla\hat{p},\nabla_{\Gamma}\hat{q})
on a set of measure zero, we get
(\nabla\hat{p},\nabla_{\Gamma}\hat{q})\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbfcal{L}^{2})
. Again, with a redefinition of
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
on a set of measure zero, we finally conclude from (
6.83
)-(
6.84
) that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in C_{w}([0,T];\mathbfcal{L}^{2}).

(6.85)

Here, we also used the fact that
(\rho(\phi),\sigma(\psi))\in C([0,T];\mathcal{L}^{2})
with
\rho\geq\rho_{\ast}>0
and
\sigma\geq\sigma_{\ast}>0
.

It remains to show that the pair
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
fulfills the initial condition

\eqref{WF:INI}_{1}
. To this end, as a direct consequence of Corollary
5.10
and (
6.14
), we first notice that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{m}(0),\mathbf{w}_{m}(0))=\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\rightarrow\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})=(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }\mathbfcal{L}^{2}

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Then, as
(\phi_{m}(0),\psi_{m}(0))=(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})
a.e. in
\Omega\times\Gamma
, it readily follows that

\displaystyle(\rho(\phi_{m}(0))\mathbf{v}_{m}(0),\sigma(\psi_{m}(0))\mathbf{w}_{m}(0))\rightarrow(\rho(\phi_{0})\mathbf{v}_{0},\sigma(\psi_{0})\mathbf{w}_{0})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }\mathbfcal{L}^{2}

(6.86)

as
m\rightarrow\infty
.
We finally conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{0})\mathbf{v}(0)\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{0})\mathbf{w}(0)\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}(\rho(\phi_{0})\mathbf{v}(0))\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi_{0})\mathbf{w}(0))\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Omega}(\rho(\phi_{m}(0))\mathbf{v}_{m}(0))\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Gamma}(\sigma(\psi_{m}(0))\mathbf{w}_{m}(0))\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)\\
&\quad=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{m}(0))\mathbf{v}_{m}(0)\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Omega}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}})\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{m}(0))\mathbf{w}_{m}(0)\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\Big)\end{split}

(6.87)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{0})\mathbf{v}_{0}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Omega}(\widehat{\mathbf{v}})\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{0})\mathbf{w}_{0}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textnormal{div}}^{\Gamma}(\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt=\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{0})\mathbf{v}_{0}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{0})\mathbf{w}_{0}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma

for all
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}}
.
Here, the second equality follows from the convergence (
6.71
) and the fourth equality follows by means of (
6.86
).
Due to (
6.4
), we are now in the setting of Lemma
6.2
and we finally conclude that
\eqref{WF:INI}_{1}
is fulfilled.

6.5  Completion of the proof

In summary, we have shown above that the sextuplet
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w},\phi,\psi,\mu,\theta)
fulfills the conditions
(i)
-
(iii)
of Definition
4.1
.
Moreover, condition
(iv)
now easily follows by testing the variational formulation (
4.7b
) with
(\zeta,\xi)\equiv(\beta,1)
if
L\in(0,\infty)
or with
(\zeta,\xi)\equiv(1,0)
and
(\zeta,\xi)\equiv(0,1)
if
L=\infty
.

To verify the energy inequality (
(v)
) stated in Definition
4.1
(v)
, we first notice that

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{tot}}(\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}),\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\to E_{\mathrm{tot}}(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\phi_{0},\psi_{0}),

(6.88)

as
m\to\infty
, which follows from the convergence of the projection
\mathbfcal{P}_{\mathbfcal{V}_{m}}
(cf. Corollary
5.10
).
Then, applying the limes inferior on both sides of (
6.3
), we use the convergences (
6.3
)-(
6.52
) and (
6.88
) along with standard convergence and weak lower semicontinuity arguments to conclude that
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w},\phi,\psi,\mu,\theta)
satisfies the energy inequality (
(v)
). For a more detailed proof in a very similar situation, we refer to
[
38
, Proof of Theorem 3.3, Step 4]
.

This means that all conditions
(i)
-
(v)
from Definition
4.1
are verified. Consequently, the sextuplet
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w},\phi,\psi,\mu,\theta)
is a weak solution in the sense of Definition
4.1
and therefore, the proof of Theorem
6.1
is complete.
∎

7  Asymptotic limit
L\rightarrow 0
and existence of a weak solution for
L=0

Lastly, in this section, we prove the existence of a weak solution to system (
4.3
) in the remaining case
L=0
.

Theorem 7.1
.

Suppose that the assumptions
(A1)
-
(A5)
hold. Let
K\in[0,\infty]
,
let
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\in\mathbfcal{L}^{2}_{\textnormal{div}}
, and let
(\phi_{0},\psi_{0})\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K,\alpha}
satisfy (
4.4a
)-(
4.4b
).
Moreover, assume that
\beta(\tilde{\sigma}_{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{1})=-(\tilde{\rho}_{2}-\tilde{\rho}_{1})
. For any
L\in(0,\infty)
, let
(\mathbf{v}_{L},\mathbf{w}_{L},\phi_{L},\psi_{L},\mu_{L},\theta_{L})
denote a weak solution to system (
4.3
) in the sense of Definition
4.1
with initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\phi_{0},\psi_{0})
. Then, there exists a sextuplet
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast},\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast},\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})
with
\mu_{\ast}=\beta\theta_{\ast}
a.e. on
\Sigma
such that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{L},\mathbf{w}_{L})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly-star in\penalty\ }L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}}),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}),

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\phi_{L},\partial_{t}\psi_{L})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\partial_{t}\phi_{\ast},\partial_{t}\psi_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{0,\beta})^{\prime}),

\displaystyle(\phi_{L},\psi_{L})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly-star in\penalty\ }L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}_{K,\alpha}^{1}),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{s})\text{\penalty\ for all\penalty\ }s\in[0,1),

\displaystyle(\mu_{L},\theta_{L})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})

as
L\rightarrow 0
, along a non-relabeled subsequence, with

\displaystyle\|\beta\theta_{L}-\mu_{L}\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}\leq C\sqrt{L},

and the sextuplet
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast},\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast},\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})
is a weak solution to system (
4.3
) in the sense of Definition
4.1
for
L=0
.

In this proof, the letter
C
will denote generic positive constants, which may depend only on
\Omega
,
T
, the initial data, and the constants introduced in
(A1)
-
(A5)
except for
L
. The exact value of
C
may vary throughout the proof.

We consider an arbitrary sequence
\{L_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\infty)
such that
L_{m}\rightarrow 0
and a corresponding weak solution
(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}},\phi_{L_{m}},\psi_{L_{m}},\mu_{L_{m}},\theta_{L_{m}})
to the initial data
(\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\phi_{0},\psi_{0})
. Let now
m\in\mathbb{N}
be arbitrary. Using the energy inequality (
(v)
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}+\|(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1})}\leq C,

(7.1)

\displaystyle\|(\phi_{L_{m}},\psi_{L_{m}})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1})}+\chi(K)^{1/2}\|\alpha\psi_{L_{m}}-\phi_{L_{m}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\mu_{L_{m}},\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{L_{m}})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})}+L_{m}^{-1/2}\|\beta\theta_{L_{m}}-\mu_{L_{m}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C.

(7.3)

In particular, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle\|\beta\theta_{L_{m}}-\mu_{L_{m}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C\sqrt{L_{m}}.

(7.4)

Next, take
(\zeta,\xi)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{0,\beta}
as a test function in (
4.7b
). This yields

\displaystyle\left|\big<(\partial_{t}\phi_{L_{m}},\partial_{t}\psi_{L_{m}}),(\zeta,\xi)\big>_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{0,\beta}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla\mu_{L_{m}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt+\|\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{L_{m}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\xi\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}.

Consequently, taking the supremum over all
(\zeta,\xi)\in\mathcal{H}^{1}_{0,\beta}
with
\|(\zeta,\xi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}\leq 1
and taking into account the estimates (
7.1
)-(
7.3
), we readily deduce that

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}\phi_{L_{m}},\partial_{t}\psi_{L_{m}})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{0,\beta})^{\prime})}\leq C.

Then, the estimates

\displaystyle\|(F^{\prime}(\phi_{L_{m}}),G^{\prime}(\psi_{L_{m}}))\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{2})}+\|(\mu_{L_{m}},\theta_{L_{m}})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{2})}\leq C

can be derived as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
. Consequently, by the Banach–Alaoglu theorem and Aubin–Lions–Simon lemma, there exists a sextuplet
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast},\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast},\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})
such that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly-star in\penalty\ }L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2}),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}}),

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\phi_{L_{m}},\partial_{t}\psi_{L_{m}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\partial_{t}\phi_{\ast},\partial_{t}\psi_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{0,\beta})^{\prime}),

\displaystyle(\phi_{L_{m}},\psi_{L_{m}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly-star in\penalty\ }L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}_{K,\alpha}),

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }C([0,T];\mathcal{H}^{s})\text{\penalty\ for all\penalty\ }s\in[0,1),

\displaystyle(\mu_{L_{m}},\theta_{L_{m}})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{H}^{1}),

\displaystyle\beta\theta_{L_{m}}-\mu_{L_{m}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\beta\theta_{\ast}-\mu_{\ast}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma)),

as
m\rightarrow\infty
, along a non-relabeled subsequence. In addition, we have

\displaystyle\|\beta\theta_{L_{m}}-\mu_{L_{m}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C\sqrt{L_{m}}\rightarrow 0

as
m\rightarrow 0
, which entails
\beta\theta_{\ast}=\mu_{\ast}
a.e. on
\Sigma
.
Moreover, using Minty’s trick to identify the limit (cf. (
6.29
)-(
6.31
)), we deduce that

\displaystyle(F^{\prime}(\phi_{L_{m}}),G^{\prime}(\psi_{L_{m}}))\rightarrow(F^{\prime}(\phi_{*}),G^{\prime}(\psi_{*}))\hskip 20.00003pt\text{weakly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{L}^{2}).

In particular, this entails that

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{*}\right|<1\hskip 10.00002pt\text{a.e.\penalty\ in\penalty\ }Q\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\left|\psi_{*}\right|<1\hskip 10.00002pt\text{a.e.\penalty\ on\penalty\ }\Sigma.

Using the above convergences, it is straightforward to pass to the limit in the weak formulations (
4.7b
) and (
4.7
) written for

(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}},\phi_{L_{m}},\psi_{L_{m}},\mu_{L_{m}},\theta_{L_{m}})

to show that the
sextuplet
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast},\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast},\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})

satisfies (
4.7b
) and (
4.7
).

Next we prove a compactness result for
\{(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. As by assumption it holds that
\beta(\tilde{\sigma}_{2}-\tilde{\sigma}_{1})=-(\tilde{\rho}_{2}-\tilde{\rho}_{1})
, the weak formulation (
4.7
) written for
(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}},\phi_{L_{m}},\psi_{L_{m}},\mu_{L_{m}},\theta_{L_{m}})
reads as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}}\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}}\cdot\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho(\phi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}}\otimes\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}}:\nabla\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sigma(\psi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}}\otimes\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}2\nu_{\Omega}(\phi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{D}\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}}:\mathbf{D}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}2\nu_{\Gamma}(\psi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}}:\mathbf{D}_{\Gamma}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\gamma(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}}\otimes\mathbf{J}_{L_{m}}:\nabla\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\qquad-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}}\otimes\mathbf{K}_{L_{m}}:\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu_{L_{m}}\nabla\phi_{L_{m}}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\;\mathrm{d}x\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\theta_{L_{m}}\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi_{L_{m}}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\;\mathrm{d}\Gamma\;\mathrm{d}t\end{split}

(7.5)

for all
(\widehat{\mathbf{v}},\widehat{\mathbf{w}})\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{2}_{0,\textnormal{div}})
, where

\mathbf{J}_{L_{m}}=-\rho^{\prime}\,\nabla\mu_{L_{m}}
and
\mathbf{K}_{L_{m}}=-\sigma^{\prime}\,\nabla_{\Gamma}\theta_{L_{m}}
.
Now, arguing as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
, we can show that

\displaystyle\{\partial_{t}\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\rho(\phi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\sigma(\psi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{is bounded in\penalty\ }L^{8/7}(0,T;(\mathbfcal{W}^{1,4}_{0,\textnormal{div}})^{\prime}).

Thus, as we additionally know that

\displaystyle\{\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\rho(\phi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\sigma(\psi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{is bounded in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}}),

it follows by the Aubin–Lions–Simon lemma that, up to subsequence extraction,

\displaystyle\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\rho(\phi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\sigma(\psi_{L_{m}})\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})\rightarrow(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})

as
m\rightarrow\infty
for some
(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;\mathbfcal{H}^{1}_{0,\textnormal{div}})
. Then, as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
, the limit can be identified as

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})=\mathbfcal{P}_{\textnormal{div}}(\rho(\phi_{\ast})\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\sigma(\psi_{\ast})\mathbf{w}_{\ast}).

Moreover, arguing as in the derivation of (
6.62
) and (
6.63
), we establish the strong convergence

\displaystyle(\mathbf{v}_{L_{m}},\mathbf{w}_{L_{m}})\rightarrow(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast})\hskip 20.00003pt\text{strongly in\penalty\ }L^{2}(0,T;\mathbfcal{L}^{2})

as
m\rightarrow\infty
. Finally, following the line of argument in the proof of Theorem
6.1
, we verify that the limit sextuplet
(\mathbf{v}_{\ast},\mathbf{w}_{\ast},\phi_{\ast},\psi_{\ast},\mu_{\ast},\theta_{\ast})
is indeed a weak solution of (
4.3
) in the sense of Definition
4.1
in the case
L=0
.
∎
